## GHSA-4xm8-rg9h-jhq2 · @signalk/resources-provider

2026-10-30 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application allowed resource IDs to contain path traversal sequences (e.g., '..'), which were then directly used in file system operations without proper sanitization or validation. This enabled an attacker to escape the intended resource directory and access arbitrary files on the server.

### Impact

An attacker could read sensitive files outside the intended resource directories, including the JWT signing secret, potentially leading to authentication bypass and remote code execution by forging JWTs.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/resources-provider-plugin/src/filestorage.ts
+++ b/packages/resources-provider-plugin/src/filestorage.ts
@@ -111,10 +120,9 @@ export class FileStore implements IResourceStore {
     property?: string
   ): Promise<Resource<object>> {
     await this.waitForInit()
+    const file = this.resourceFile(type, itemUuid)
     try {
-      let result = JSON.parse(
-        await readFile(path.join(this.resources[type].path, itemUuid), 'utf8')
-      )
+      let result = JSON.parse(await readFile(file, 'utf8'))
       if (property) {
         const value = property.split('.').reduce((acc, val) => {
           return acc[val]
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `isSafeCollectionName` function and a `resourceFile` method to strictly validate resource IDs and types, ensuring they do not contain path traversal characters or sequences. It also uses `path.resolve` and checks if the resolved file path remains within the expected directory, preventing directory traversal.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 100 occurrences**

## GHSA-c73q-w75p-j82v · Authlib

2026-10-30 · Python · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The original implementation of the RFC 7592 client configuration endpoint in Authlib did not bind the registration access token to the specific client it was issued for. This oversight meant that any valid registration access token could be used to access or modify *any* client's configuration, not just the one it was intended for.

### Impact

An attacker with a valid registration access token for one client could potentially access, modify, or delete the configuration of any other client registered on the server, leading to unauthorized access, data manipulation, or denial of service for other clients.

### Key diff

```
--- a/authlib/oauth2/rfc7592/endpoint.py
+++ b/authlib/oauth2/rfc7592/endpoint.py
@@ -39,6 +39,11 @@ def create_configuration_response(self, request):
                 status_code=401, description="The client does not exist on this server."
             )
 
+        # The registration access token is only valid for the client it was
+        # issued to.
+        if not token.check_client(client):
+            raise AccessDeniedError()
+
         if not self.check_permission(client, request):
             # If the client does not have permission to read its record, the server
             # MUST return an HTTP 403 Forbidden.
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a crucial check within the `create_configuration_response` method. It now explicitly verifies that the authenticated registration access token belongs to the client being requested, preventing tokens from being used across different clients. It also updates documentation to reflect this requirement.

## GHSA-2qm8-x737-gmw5 · pyasn1

2026-10-30 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an inefficient algorithm used to decode constructed BIT STRINGs. When concatenating multiple BIT STRING fragments, the original implementation repeatedly created new `BitString` objects and copied existing data, leading to quadratic time complexity with respect to the number of fragments or total length.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious ASN.1 payload with many small BIT STRING fragments. Decoding this payload would consume excessive CPU resources, potentially leading to a denial of service for applications processing such data.

### Key diff

```
-            bitString = self.protoComponent.fromOctetString(
-                component[1:], internalFormat=True,
-                prepend=bitString, padding=trailingBits
-            )
+            bitLength = self._appendFragment(assembled, component, bitLength)
+
+        bitString = self.protoComponent.fromOctetString(
+            assembled, internalFormat=True, padding=(-bitLength) % 8
+        )
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new static method `_appendFragment` that efficiently appends BIT STRING fragments to a `bytearray`. Instead of repeatedly creating new `BitString` objects, it now builds the complete BIT STRING in a `bytearray` and then converts it to a `BitString` object once, changing the complexity from quadratic to linear.

## GHSA-4528-x5fx-h6hg · joi

2026-10-30 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The previous regular expression used for splitting email addresses in `string().email({ multiple: true })` could lead to quadratic time complexity when processing long strings with many whitespace characters. This was due to the regex allowing repeated leading whitespace matches, causing excessive backtracking.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted input string with many whitespace characters, causing the server to spend excessive CPU time processing the input, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
-	const regex = new RegExp(`\s*[${options.separator ? escapeRegex(options.separator) : ','}]\s*`);
+	const regex = options.multiple ? new RegExp(`(?:(?<!\s)\s+)?[${options.separator ? escapeRegex(options.separator) : ','}]\s*`) : undefined;
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the regular expression used for splitting email addresses when the `multiple: true` option is enabled. It introduces a non-capturing group with a negative lookbehind assertion `(?:(?<!\s)\s+)` to ensure that leading whitespace is only matched at the beginning of a run, preventing quadratic time complexity.

## GHSA-8fmv-6rg5-5924 · github.com/kubeflow/pipelines

2026-10-30 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application processed archive files (tar.gz, zip) without proper limits on the decompressed size or the total amount of data read during decompression. An attacker could craft a 'decompression bomb' archive, which is a small compressed file that expands into a very large amount of data, leading to excessive memory consumption.

### Impact

An attacker could exhaust the server's memory resources by uploading a specially crafted archive file, causing the application to crash or become unresponsive, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/src/apiserver/server/util.go
+++ b/backend/src/apiserver/server/util.go
@@ -17,31 +17,21 @@ package server
 	"archive/tar"
 	"archive/zip"
-	"bufio"
 	"bytes"
 	"compress/gzip"
 	"errors"
 	"io"
 	"strings"
 
+	"github.com/kubeflow/pipelines/backend/src/apiserver/common"
 	"github.com/kubeflow/pipelines/backend/src/common/util"
 )
 
 func loadFile(fileReader io.Reader, MaxFileLength int) ([]byte, error) {
-	// TODO(lingqinggan): investigate ways to increase the buffer size, so we don't have to use a loop.
-	reader := bufio.NewReaderSize(fileReader, MaxFileLength)
-	var pipelineFile []byte
-	for {
-		currentRead := make([]byte, bufio.MaxScanTokenSize)
-		size, err := reader.Read(currentRead)
-		pipelineFile = append(pipelineFile, currentRead[:size]...)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `io.LimitReader` to restrict the total bytes read during file loading and archive decompression (both tar.gz and zip). It also adds a 'traversal budget' for tarball decompression to account for metadata and prevent excessive processing of archive entries, ensuring that the decompressed content and overall archive processing stay within defined limits.

## GHSA-jm85-8cpp-9cjc · adminneo-org/adminneo

2026-10-30 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application stored version information in a serialized file without proper validation of the input. An attacker could send a crafted POST request to the 'script=version' endpoint with an array as the 'version' parameter, which would then be serialized and stored in the 'adminneo.version' file. Subsequent attempts to unserialize this malformed data would fail, leading to a persistent denial of service.

### Impact

An attacker could cause a persistent denial of service, preventing legitimate users from accessing the AdminNeo interface due to the application's inability to process the corrupted version file.

### Key diff

```
--- a/admin/include/bootstrap.inc.php
+++ b/admin/include/bootstrap.inc.php
@@ -41,17 +41,6 @@
 // Compiled files loading.
 include __DIR__ . "/../file.inc.php";
 
-if ($_GET["script"] == "version") {
-	$filename = get_temp_dir() . "/adminneo.version";
-	@unlink($filename); // It may not be writable by us, @ - it may not exist.
-
-	$file = open_file_with_lock($filename);
-	if ($file) {
-		write_and_unlock_file($file, serialize(["version" => $_POST["version"]]));
-	}
-	exit;
-}
-
 if (!$_SERVER["REQUEST_URI"]) { // IIS 5 compatibility
 	$_SERVER["REQUEST_URI"] = $_SERVER["ORIG_PATH_INFO"];
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the functionality that saves the version information to a serialized file based on user input. It also removes the code that attempts to load and unserialize this file. The 'adminneo.version' file is now explicitly unlinked.

## GHSA-jpxq-7h8p-2q76 · joserfc

2026-10-30 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application processed an unbounded number of recipients in a JWE General JSON Serialization. Each recipient required a private-key decryption operation, which is computationally expensive. An attacker could craft a JWE with a large number of recipients, leading to excessive CPU usage and resource exhaustion.

### Impact

An attacker could cause a denial of service by sending a maliciously crafted JWE, preventing legitimate users from accessing the service and potentially crashing the server due to CPU exhaustion.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/joserfc/_rfc7516/registry.py
+++ b/src/joserfc/_rfc7516/registry.py
@@ -62,6 +63,8 @@ class JWERegistry:
     max_ciphertext_length: int = 65536  # 64KB
     #: max auth tag's size in bytes
     max_auth_tag_length: int = 64
+    #: max number of recipients in JWE General JSON Serialization
+    max_recipients: int = 16
 
     def __init__(
         self,
@@ -122,6 +128,10 @@ def validate_auth_tag_size(self, tag: bytes) -> None:
         if tag and len(tag) > self.max_auth_tag_length:
             raise ExceededSizeError(f"Auth tag size exceeds {self.max_auth_tag_length} bytes.")
 
+    def validate_recipient_count(self, recipients: Collection[t.Any]) -> None:
+        if len(recipients) > self.max_recipients:
+            raise ExceededSizeError(f"Recipient count exceeds {self.max_recipients}.")
 
--- a/src/joserfc/_rfc7516/json.py
+++ b/src/joserfc/_rfc7516/json.py
@@ -76,12 +76,14 @@ def extract_general_json(data: GeneralJSONSerialization, registry: JWERegistry)
     protected = json_b64decode(protected_segment)
 
     unprotected = data.get("unprotected")
+    recipients = data["recipients"]
+    registry.validate_recipient_count(recipients)
     base64_segments, bytes_segments, aad = __extract_segments(data, registry)
 
     obj = GeneralJSONEncryption(protected, None, unprotected, aad)
     obj.base64_segments = base64_segments
     obj.bytes_segments = bytes_segments
-    for item in data["recipients"]:
+    for item in recipients:
         recipient = __extract_recipient(obj, item, registry)
         obj.recipients.append(recipient)
     return obj
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `max_recipients` configuration option, defaulting to 16, to limit the number of recipients allowed in a JWE General JSON Serialization. A new validation method `validate_recipient_count` is added to enforce this limit, raising an `ExceededSizeError` if the count is surpassed.

## GHSA-f3fj-9625-rv3m · github.com/charmbracelet/soft-serve

2026-10-30 · Go · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `RACE_CONDITION→TOCTOU`

### Root cause

The original code checked the repository's private status and sent a webhook event after potentially modifying the private status. However, it didn't explicitly track if the private status actually changed. If the `SetPrivate` function was called with the same `private` value that the repository already had, a race condition could occur where the `git-daemon-export-ok` file (which controls public access) might be created or deleted by another concurrent operation, leading to an inconsistent state where a private repository could be temporarily readable by unauthenticated users.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially exploit this race condition to briefly access the contents of a private repository without authentication, leading to unauthorized information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/backend/repo.go
+++ b/pkg/backend/repo.go
@@ -664,8 +664,15 @@ func (d *Backend) SetPrivate(ctx context.Context, name string, private bool) err
 	name = utils.SanitizeRepo(name)
 	rp := filepath.Join(d.repoPath(name))
 
+	var changed bool
 	if err := db.WrapError(
 		d.db.TransactionContext(ctx, func(tx *db.Tx) error {
+			m, err := d.store.GetRepoByName(ctx, tx, name)
+			if err != nil {
+				return err
+			}
+			changed = m.Private != private
+
 			fp := filepath.Join(rp, "git-daemon-export-ok")
 			if !private {
 				if err := os.WriteFile(fp, []byte{}, fs.ModePerm); err != nil {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `changed` boolean variable to explicitly track if the private status of the repository was actually modified during the transaction. The webhook event is now only sent if the `private` status has genuinely changed, preventing unnecessary operations and ensuring consistency in the repository's access state.

## GHSA-mrwp-f3q8-3g9p · follow-redirects

2026-10-30 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 5.9 · Pattern: `TYPE_CONFUSION→BYPASS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability arose because the `isArray` helper function used `instanceof Array` to check if `options.sensitiveHeaders` was an array. This check fails across different JavaScript realms (e.g., if the array was created in an iframe or a different Node.js context), causing the `sensitiveHeaders` array to be incorrectly discarded. Consequently, sensitive headers that should have been removed during redirects were not, leading to their exposure.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially cause sensitive headers, such as `Authorization` or `Cookie` headers, to be sent to unintended third-party sites during a redirect. This could lead to information disclosure, session hijacking, or unauthorized access to user accounts.

### Key diff

```
--- a/index.js
+++ b/index.js
@@ -311,7 +311,7 @@ RedirectableRequest.prototype._sanitizeOptions = function (options) {
     }
   }
   // Ensure sensitive headers are always present
-  if (!isArray(options.sensitiveHeaders)) {
+  if (!Array.isArray(options.sensitiveHeaders)) {
     options.sensitiveHeaders = [];
   }
 
@@ -689,10 +689,6 @@ function isSubdomain(subdomain, domain) {
   return dot > 0 && subdomain[dot] === "." && subdomain.endsWith(domain);
 }
 
-function isArray(value) {
-  return value instanceof Array;
-}
-
 function isString(value) {
   return typeof value === "string" || value instanceof String;
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the custom `isArray` helper function with the built-in and more robust `Array.isArray()` method. This ensures that array checks work correctly across different JavaScript realms, preventing the `sensitiveHeaders` array from being inadvertently discarded and ensuring sensitive headers are properly handled during redirects.

## GHSA-fgxm-5hvv-x4vh · github.com/charmbracelet/soft-serve

2026-10-30 · Go · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The application's 'go-get' handler, which is used by `go get` commands to fetch repository metadata, was not properly enforcing authorization. It was using a `withAccess` middleware that allowed all 'go-get' requests to pass through if the access level was at least 'ReadOnlyAccess', even for private repositories. This meant that an unauthenticated user could query for private repositories and receive a 200 OK response, confirming their existence and names.

### Impact

An attacker could enumerate all private repositories and their names on the server without any authentication. This could lead to further targeted attacks or simply expose sensitive project information.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/web/git.go
+++ b/pkg/web/git.go
@@ -113,7 +113,7 @@ func GitController(_ context.Context, r *mux.Router) {
 
 	// Handle go-get
-	r.Handle(basePrefix, withParams(withAccess(http.HandlerFunc(GoGetHandler)))).Methods(http.MethodGet)
+	r.Handle(basePrefix, withParams(withCaller(http.HandlerFunc(GoGetHandler)))).Methods(http.MethodGet)
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the authentication and authorization middleware. It introduces a new `withCaller` middleware that authenticates the user but does not authorize repository access, and modifies `withAccess` to explicitly check for repository access. The 'go-get' handler now uses `withCaller` instead of `withAccess`, and explicitly checks the user's access level to the repository before rendering the go-get template, preventing disclosure of private repository information.

## GHSA-42gh-23hr-vphw · pypdf

2026-10-30 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The previous implementation of `page_labels` iterated through each page and called `index2label` for every single page. The `index2label` function itself could involve traversing a number tree structure from its root for each call, leading to redundant and inefficient processing, especially for documents with many pages and complex page label structures.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a PDF document with a large number of pages or a complex page label structure, causing the `page_labels` property to take an excessively long time to compute. This could lead to a denial of service for applications processing such PDFs, consuming significant CPU resources and memory.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/_doc_common.py
+++ b/pypdf/_doc_common.py
@@ -1295,7 +1295,7 @@ def page_labels(self) -> list[str]:
         This property is read-only. The labels are in the order that the pages
         appear in the document.
         """
-        return [page_index2page_label(self, i) for i in range(len(self.pages))]
+        return index2labels(self, len(self.pages))
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function `index2labels` that optimizes the retrieval of page labels. It first checks if the `/Nums` array in the `/PageLabels` dictionary is a flat array with increasing integer keys and direct values. If so, it performs a single pass to collect all labels efficiently. Otherwise, it falls back to the page-by-page approach, but the optimization significantly reduces the worst-case runtime for common scenarios.

## GHSA-5849-834g-hvw5 · pypdf

2026-10-30 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The PDF parser could enter an infinite loop when processing a malformed PDF file. Specifically, during the search for the 'trailer' keyword in the standard xref table, if the stream repeatedly returned to the same position without finding the 'trailer' tag, the loop would never terminate.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious PDF file that, when parsed by pypdf, would cause the application to hang indefinitely, leading to a denial of service for any process attempting to read the document.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/_reader.py
+++ b/pypdf/_reader.py
@@ -1072,6 +1072,7 @@ def _read_standard_xref_table(self, stream: StreamType) -> None:
         stream.seek(-1, 1)
         first_time = True  # check if the first time looking at the xref table
         recovery_cache: Optional[dict[int, tuple[int, int]]] = None
+        previous_position: Optional[int] = None
         while True:
             num = cast(int, read_object(stream, self))
             if first_time and num != 0:
@@ -1186,12 +1187,20 @@ def _read_standard_xref_table(self, stream: StreamType) -> None:
                 stream.seek(-1, 1)
             stream.seek(-1, 1)
             trailer_tag = stream.read(7)
-            if trailer_tag != b"trailer":
-                # more xrefs!
-                stream.seek(-7, 1)
-            else:
+            if trailer_tag == b"trailer":
                 break
 
+            # More xrefs should move the parser to another section.
+            stream.seek(-7, 1);
+
+            # Detect a repeated read at the same position.
+            rewound_position = stream.tell();
+            if rewound_position == previous_position:
+                raise PdfReadError(
+                    f"Xref parsing stuck at position {rewound_position}: {trailer_tag!r}"
+                )
+            previous_position = rewound_position
 
     def _read_xref_tables_and_trailers(
         self, stream: StreamType, startxref: Optional[int], xref_issue_nr: int
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a mechanism to detect if the parser gets stuck in a loop while searching for the 'trailer' keyword. It tracks the previous stream position and raises a PdfReadError if the parser attempts to read from the same position repeatedly, thus preventing an infinite loop.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 35 occurrences**

## GHSA-9pg5-8c7x-5hfx · joserfc

2026-10-30 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application would allocate memory for incoming JWS/JWE compact serialized values before performing a size check. An attacker could send a very large, malformed JWS/JWE string, causing the application to allocate significant memory unnecessarily.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger excessive memory allocation, potentially leading to a denial of service by exhausting system resources or causing the application to crash.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/joserfc/_rfc7797/compact.py
+++ b/src/joserfc/_rfc7797/compact.py
@@ -37,13 +37,15 @@ def extract_rfc7515_compact(
     if registry is None:
         registry = default_registry
 
+    registry.validate_compact_value_size(value)
+
     parts = value.split(b".")
     if len(parts) != 3:
         raise DecodeError("Invalid JSON Web Signature")
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `validate_compact_value_size` methods in both JWS and JWE registries. These methods perform an early check on the total length of the compact serialized value against a predefined maximum length before any parsing or segment allocation occurs.

## GHSA-cggf-277g-3q54 · pypdf

2026-10-30 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The pypdf library did not limit the total number of page dictionary entries copied when flattening a PDF's page tree. Maliciously crafted PDFs with a large number of page entries, potentially through repeated references to the same page, could cause excessive memory consumption.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a PDF file that, when parsed by pypdf, would consume an inordinate amount of memory, leading to a denial of service for applications processing such files.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/_doc_common.py
+++ b/pypdf/_doc_common.py
@@ -1389,6 +1389,7 @@ def _flatten(self, list_only: bool = False) -> None:
 
         flattened_pages: list[PageObject] = []
         traversal_state = _TraversalState()
+        remaining_page_entries = _TraversalState(entry_count=configuration.page_tree_maximum_page_entries)
         # id() values of the nodes on the path from the root to the current node.
         # Detects multi-hop cycles such as A→B→C→A that the single-parent check misses.
         ancestor_ids: set[int] = set()
@@ -1421,7 +1422,11 @@ def _flatten(self, list_only: bool = False) -> None:
                 ancestor_ids.add(id(node))
                 stack.append(self._enter_page_tree_node(item))
             elif node_type == "/Page":
-                flattened_pages.append(self._flatten_leaf_page(item, list_only))
+                flattened_pages.append(
+                    self._flatten_leaf_page(
+                        item=item, list_only=list_only, remaining_page_entries=remaining_page_entries
+                    )
+                )
 
             item = self._pop_next_page_tree_kid(stack, ancestor_ids, traversal_state)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new configuration parameter, `page_tree_maximum_page_entries`, which sets a limit on the total number of page dictionary entries copied during page tree flattening. It also adds logic to track and decrement this count, raising a `LimitReachedError` if the limit is exceeded.

## GHSA-mfr5-77xh-9j7h · pypdf

2026-10-30 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the logic for detecting already visited objects during dictionary cloning incorrectly used the indirect reference of the *cloned* object instead of the *source* object. If a cloned object did not have an indirect reference (e.g., it was a direct object), it would never be marked as visited, leading to an infinite loop if the dictionary contained circular references.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a PDF document with specific dictionary structures that, when processed by pypdf, would cause an infinite loop, leading to a denial of service for any application attempting to process such a document.

### Key diff

```
source_reference = cur_obj.indirect_reference
                             clon = cast(
                                 "DictionaryObject",
-                                clon.indirect_reference is not None:
-                                idnum = clon.indirect_reference.idnum
-                                generation = clon.indirect_reference.generation
+                                source_reference is not None:
+                                source_idnum = source_reference.idnum
+                                source_generation = source_reference.generation
```

### Fix summary

The patch corrects the logic for tracking visited objects during dictionary cloning. It now stores the indirect reference of the *source* object before cloning, ensuring that the correct object is added to the 'visited' set and preventing infinite loops when encountering circular references.

## GHSA-qm8w-vx34-5j9j · @nx/gradle

2026-10-30 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the way command-line arguments, particularly those originating from `nx.json` plugin options, were handled when spawning child processes on Windows. Specifically, special shell characters like `&` in arguments were not properly quoted or escaped, leading to their interpretation as command separators by the Windows command shell (`cmd.exe`) when `shell: true` was implicitly or explicitly used.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary OS commands by crafting malicious input within `nx.json` plugin options. These commands would then be executed on the victim's machine, potentially leading to remote code execution, data exfiltration, or system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/nx/src/utils/safe-spawn.spec.ts
+++ b/packages/nx/src/utils/safe-spawn.spec.ts
@@ -107,6 +107,16 @@
     );
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `safeSpawn` and `safeExecFileSync` utilities that carefully handle command execution, especially on Windows. It implements robust quoting mechanisms for binary paths and arguments containing special shell characters (like `&` and spaces) when a shell is used. It also explicitly refuses arguments or binary paths containing line breaks or double quotes on Windows to prevent command injection, while ensuring that arguments are passed literally without a shell on non-Windows platforms.

## GHSA-vp5q-ff62-jwqv · russh

2026-10-30 · RUST · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The `russh` agent server did not correctly implement the `Agent::confirm` mechanism. When a key was added with the confirmation constraint (`ssh-add -c`), the server would sign requests without actually prompting for or receiving user confirmation, effectively bypassing the intended security measure.

### Impact

An attacker who gains control of the SSH agent could use keys that were intended to require confirmation for signing operations without the user's explicit approval, potentially leading to unauthorized access or actions.

### Key diff

```
--- a/russh/src/server/mod.rs
+++ b/russh/src/server/mod.rs
@@ -194,8 +193,9 @@ pub enum Auth {
 
 /// Server handler. Each client will have their own handler.
 ///
-/// Note: this is an `async_trait`. Click `[source]` on the right to see actual async function definitions.
-#[async_trait]
+/// Note: this is an async trait. The trait functions return `impl Future`,
+/// and you can simply define them as `async fn` instead.
+#[cfg_attr(feature = "async-trait", async_trait::async_trait)]
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `Handler` trait in `russh/src/server/mod.rs` to no longer rely on `async_trait` for its async functions. Instead, it explicitly defines these functions to return `impl Future`, which allows for more flexible and correct implementation of asynchronous logic, including the `Agent::confirm` call.

## GHSA-x3rh-gjgm-fmqc · pypdf

2026-10-30 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The pypdf library did not limit the number of entries in cross-reference streams within PDF documents. An attacker could craft a PDF with an excessively large number of entries, causing the parser to consume significant memory and CPU resources when processing the document.

### Impact

An attacker could cause a denial of service by providing a specially crafted PDF file, leading to high memory consumption and potential application crashes or unresponsiveness for services processing such documents.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/_reader.py
+++ b/pypdf/_reader.py
@@ -129,6 +131,7 @@ def __init__(
         password: Union[str, bytes, None] = None,
         *,
         root_object_recovery_limit: Optional[int] = 10_000,
+        xref_stream_maximum_entries: int = 2_000_000,
     ) -> None:
         self.strict = strict
         self.flattened_pages: Optional[list[PageObject]] = None
@@ -147,6 +150,8 @@ def __init__(
         self._root_object_recovery_limit = (
             root_object_recovery_limit if isinstance(root_object_recovery_limit, int) else sys.maxsize
         )
+        self._xref_stream_maximum_entries = xref_stream_maximum_entries
+        self._xref_stream_entry_count = 0
         apply_legacy_configuration()
 
         # Map page indirect_reference number to page number
@@ -1395,6 +1400,13 @@ def _read_pdf15_xref_stream(self, stream: StreamType) -> StreamObject:
             index_pairs=index_pairs, entry_sizes=entry_sizes, xref_stream=xref_stream
         )
 
+        self._xref_stream_entry_count += sum(index_pairs[1::2])
+        if self._xref_stream_entry_count > self._xref_stream_maximum_entries:
+            raise LimitReachedError(
+                f"Maximum XRef stream entry limit reached: "
+                f"{self._xref_stream_entry_count} > {self._xref_stream_maximum_entries}."
+            )
+
         stream_data = BytesIO(xref_stream.get_data())
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new configuration parameter, `xref_stream_maximum_entries`, to limit the total number of entries allowed in stream-based cross-reference tables. It also adds a counter to track the number of entries and raises a `LimitReachedError` if the maximum is exceeded, preventing excessive resource usage.
